Editorial · page 3

Tighten your belts

We are pleased to report that Tilda Rice have agreed to extend their sponsorship of Belgrave Harriers for a further 12 months although we are sorry that their deal with Du'aine Ladejo has now run it's course. The main reason for Tilda's continued support of the Club is their recognition of the enormous efforts we have made in taking athletics to young people and the schools and encouraging them to become involved in our beloved sport. Sadly the extension is only for one year and furthermore Sportsmatch, who initially matched Tilda's sponsorship pound for pound, have now reduced their support to only 25%. Basically, therefore, we have £12,500 to continue our Youth Scheme into 1997. It might seem like quite a tidy sum but when you consider that this summer we may well make the National Young Athletes Final, involving the transportation of around 80 young athletes to Birmingham, we have a Women's Southern League match in Newquay which may again result in a large group of youngsters staying away from home overnight, and we have some fifteen away matches in various leagues which will require coach travel at around £250 per time, you can see that it is not going to go very far. Our crusade to carry athletics into the local schools week in week out will also take its toll but we are committed to doing this otherwise we will not earn even the aforementioned sponsorship. All this at a time when our subscriptions seem to be slower and slower in coming forth and the total received doesn't come anywhere near covering the cost of the normal activities that any athletic club is involved with. Additionally we have the ongoing cost of maintaining our Club Headquarters.

Our financial wizards meet regularly to keep an eye on our affairs and at their most recent gathering went through the budgets for each section of our club with a fine tooth comb. It became quite obvious that if we don't come up with something soon then some cuts are going to have to be made - deep cuts which will probably affect every activity covered by Belgrave Harriers.

So, the message is that we are all going to have to reduce our spending. Everyone will have to chip in more generously when it comes to collecting fares on coaches and mini-buses. Club kit needs to be paid for up front. Every effort must be made to reduce our spending in every area and even this very magazine may have to take on some other form. We are going to have to tighten our belts.

Du'aine Ladejo

Feature · page 3

Words are not enough

For nearly thirty years Leo Coy has been at the heart of our affairs, first as a runner and then as a team manager as we made our way up the ladders of both British and Young Athletes Leagues. Every Belgrave social gathering - from camping expeditions in the New Forest to Club Dinners has benefited from his presence. It hasn't all been just fair-weather support either. In the depths of winter young lads have been collected from all points of South London and driven to training and competition venues for cross country and road running; he has stood at the trackside all year round supervising warm-up drills, timing repetitions and coaching high-jump. He has been involved in everything we have done during three decades and of course became our Club President for 1988-89. Great success has come our way, due in no small part to Leo's efforts but he has admitted that all the hard work has been repaid by seeing our young athletes take their first competitive steps as eleven and twelve year-olds in the Thameside competitions - and finally gain selection for the British League team.

All things must pass, however. Gradually over the last year Leo has been easing his way out of his club responsibilities, paving the way for a planned move back to his native Ireland. At first we didn't really believe he would carry it through but we should have known better. By Springtime Leo will have taken up residence in County Wexford and no more will we be able to rely on his efforts as a matter of course. He assures us that he will continue to make his way to Club functions and that we will see him cheering on our teams at venues around the country. We look forward to those occasions Leo but in the meantime we wish you every happiness in your new home - and mere words, written or spoken, cannot thank you enough for all you have done.

Leo Coy

Back page photo

Back page photo by Robin Nicholls. Half of the awesome foursome. Our Junior Men cracked the 4x100m, 4x200m and 4x400m Club Records in 1996 and these two had a hand in every one of those performances. Richard David, passing the baton, joined us at 15 years of age and last season, at 19, set pbs of 10.98, 22.1 and 48.7. Seventeen years-old Marlon Dickson has been with us since he was 12 and this year has run 10.65, 21.6 and a 49.2 relay leg.

Notice · page 4

A celebration of service

Not only did Bill Lucas reach his 80th birthday a short while back but he's just completed 61 years of dedicated service to Belgrave Harriers. As fit as ever, thanks to his passion for golf, Bill's commitment to the Club is ongoing. It's almost unknown for him to miss a Committee Meeting, he is always on hand for our Finance Meetings and of course he always has a viewpoint on all our club matters. We don't intend to let a couple of anniversaries like these slip past without something special so a celebration is being planned for April 19th at Belgrave Hall. Contact Gordon Biscoe ([phone number withheld]) or Charlie Manning ([phone number withheld]) to find out more.

Bill LucasGordon BiscoeCharlie Manning

Report · page 4

Ridgeon heads '97 Club 800m rankings after "Fun Run"

The 'race' had been talked about for months - an 800 metres - just for fun - featuring some of the top 400 boys. Roger Black might be tops over one lap - but who could hold it together best for TWO laps of the track? Eventually a date and place were set - Saturday 18th January at Battersea Park. Matthew Fraser-Moat of the BMC promoted the event and in turn asked Belgrave to provide the officials. Unfortunately 'Bamby' Black was having a course of knee treatment so it was left to Mark Richardson, Vince Rose and Jon Ridgeon to take on the challenge. Jon's coach Mike Whittingham turned out to watch the fun as did Peter and Mrs. Coe, while Frank Horwill was busy on the mike keeping everyone informed about recent form and best times. The weather was mild, the track was damp and the sun even broke through the January clouds for a few minutes. With nerves jangling the sprinters lined up to race the only two genuine middle-distance men in Thompson of Dartford (best of 1:50.1) and Clive Gilby (1:47.33).

Snowy Brooks fired the starter's pistol and they were away. The pacemaker was aiming for times of 27, 54 and 82 and although bell time was just about right - 54.7 - the initial rush had been a bit ferocious so it was going to be a hard run for the sprinters. As it turned out the specialists had the race to themselves. The Valley's Vince Rose seemed to have 3rd sewn up until a tremendous dash down the straight topped off with a real sprint hurdler's dip saw Ridgeon just snatch the 'real race' on the line. With Richardson also under the qualifying time the BMC suddenly found itself with three new members.

Seriously though, put him in the right race in early track season and that nice Mr. Ridgeon might just nip under 1:50.

Result:

Result:
1,Jason Thompson (Dartford)1:51.8
2,Clive Gilby (BMC)1:52.1
3,Jon Ridgeon (Belgrave)1:54.7
4,Vince Rose (Thames Valley)1:54.7
5,Mark Richardson (Windsor, S&E)1:55.9
6,Kermit Bentham (Thames Valley)1:56.7
7,Noel Levy (Belgrave)1:59.4

Column · page 5

From The Belgravian

25 Years Ago ... 1971/72

LAURIE O'HARA has been in great form lately, and he skated away with our 5,000m championship in the fastish time of 15m 51.8s. Gerry North had to draw on his well-known ability to hold off Guy Monnet for second place ... Guy in turn had to show his sprinting powers to hold off the defending champion, Lionel Mann. Laurie ... was an easy winner of the 10,000m championship - adding the Tom Carter Trophy to the Griffen Cup. Last year's winner Lionel Mann was his closest challenger, but headed Adrian Major by only a few yards.

SURREY CC LEAGUE Division One. So, Belgrave took their 7th successive league title by clinching the final race at Petersham on February 19th. But an ominous cloud has appeared on the horizon in the form of the young Aldershot, Farnham & District contingent. This was their first year in Division 1 and they showed flashes of brilliance, particularly in the second race, which indicated that Belgrave will have to be on their toes next season if they want to make it eight in a row. 1, Belgrave H (168, 252, 276, 235) 931pts; 2, Aldershot, Farnham & Dist. (394, 173, 244, 284) 1095pts; 3, Hercules Wimbledon (292, 414, 242, 494) 1442pts. [Editors' note: in fact this proved to be the last time that Belgrave were Division One Champions.]

JOHN MOULLIN joined the ranks of the Centurions in a most spectacular fashion. With a truly magnificent display of fast, controlled walking, he finished first in the [Surrey W.C.'s Open 100 miles] race, with a remarkable time of 16hrs. 55m. 44s., only 1m. 29s. outside Frank O'Reilly's world best performance for 100 miles. ... A search through the Centurions' roll of honour shows that no one has entered their ranks with such a high class performance. To get inside 17hrs. at one's first attempt at the distance is indeed an unique achievement which makes John Moullin one of our top ultra-long-distance walkers.

and, 50 Years Ago ... 1946/47

NUMBER 100. We are all rather inclined to accept an issue of The Belgravian when it arrives as something that just "happened," and seldom do we think of the hours of preparation that go into each issue, or the care and attention which somebody must devote to every sentence on every page before it passes into print. This issue of The Belgravian is No. 100, and an appropriate moment for wishing ourselves an ever-increasing measure of success for record in the next hundred issues ...

WE ARE NOW at the end of what can be termed the first post-war winter season. From the afternoon of the season's opening meeting at Belgrave Hall the atmosphere was set: time seemed to have slipped back and the years of the war became, in many minds, non-existent. As one who "arrived back" that afternoon, not having been near Belgrave Hall since 1940, it was a startling joy to see again so many well-remembered faces and to be greeted with such a warmth of welcome. Many things have changed these past seven years and this club has lost some of its dearest and best members, but we are still a club with enthusiasm and unity about us.

SOUTH OF THAMES "JUNIOR" RACE. Held at Nonsuch Park, Cheam on November 9th, a field of 209 runners, representing 29 teams, fought out a closely-contested race over a two-lap course. As winners of the race last season we thus had to find a new scoring team, and our hopes received a set-back when Vic Blowfield failed to arrive in time. However, at the half-way stage in the race, Bill Lucas and Len Marchant were in the leading bunch of four runners, which was a good start-off to our team's chances. When they came in sight of the finish Bill Lucas was clear away in front and in 2nd position came Len Marchant, a fine performance by both runners. 1, W.Lucas 26:55; 2, L.Marchant 27:03; 15, S.Jarlett 27:55; 23, L.Bishop 28:16. Teams: 1, Belgrave H 41; 2, Herne Hill 48.

Laurie O'HaraGerry NorthGuy MonnetLionel MannAdrian MajorJohn MoullinFrank O'ReillyVic BlowfieldBill LucasLen MarchantS. JarlettL. Bishop

Letter · page 5

A message from The President

Ted Pallant

May I express my sincere thanks to everyone for my election as President. I am very proud of this honour and feel sure that we can look forward to another successful year.

On your behalf I would also like to say thank you to the retiring President Snowy Brooks. During his year of office he worked untiringly, setting a fine example in the true Belgravian tradition. It was a year to remember, Snowy, and I know you are now looking forward to competing once again in the World Veterans Decathlon Championships in 1997. We all wish you every success.

Looking ahead, the hope is to see us consolidating on the fine progress made in recent years. Much work has still to be carrried out and we must be prepared to adapt to changes in the sport if we are to stay at the top level. Fortunately we have a fine band of Officers and Coaches of high standards. They have been prepared to give their time and experience throughout the years but more help is always needed. With the ever-increasing workload of a club the size of ours, volunteers will always be welcome. Club Team Managers will also appreciate your support when it comes to turning out our strongest teams in competition. This makes all the difference between just losing a championship and winning.

Finally I would like to thank all members in the Club who have made my membership of nearly 40 years such a happy time. I look back with pleasure on the friendships formed and hope that the future will bring many more.

Best wishes,

Ted Pallant

Ted PallantSnowy Brooks

Feature · page 6

Ted Pallant: President of Belgrave Harriers 1996-1997

Charlie Dickinson

"A real surprise, I was delighted". This was Ted Pallant's reaction to being nominated as this year's President. It was less of a surprise to many in the club who knew Ted to be experienced, courteous, wise and mature, all qualities of a good President.

An active member since 1957 both as an ordinary club runner and a member of the committee, he is perhaps best know by the younger members as the person who sells the club kit. Few Belgrave members over the last five years will not have been approached by Ted and politely asked if they need a vest or a new tracksuit. This has put Ted in touch with all sections of the club and given him an overview few can match.

Born at St George's Hospital, Hyde Park in 1929, Ted spent his childhood in Battersea, living in Warriner Gardens just a stone's throw from the park. The park became Ted's playground, almost a second home, and the track was to influence him to the present day. At that time, of course, the Battersea track was cinders and three laps to the mile. The 100 yards was run parallel to the power station with taped lanes, and the long jump pit was where the tennis courts are today. Ted first competed there at the age of eight representing his Primary school, Ethelburger, in the Battersea school sports. He remembers digging his starting blocks with a trowel.

The church also played an important part in Ted's childhood. His family attended St Mary le Park Church near Albert Bridge. There were social activities, the youth club, dances, the cubs and later scouts. Ted made many friends and was to remain in the scout movement as a rover scout until the age of 28.

The war came when Ted was ten. He was evacuated from Clapham Junction station on September 3rd 1939 and war was declared while he was on the train to Reading. Strangely, it was here that Ted was to find another connection with athletics and another important influence. He was billeted with the family of the British long jump champion, W.E.N. Breach, who had competed in an international with Germany just a week before war broke out. Later, Breach was to become President of Reading AC.

Ted returned to London after only a few months and spent the war years living in Balham. He became a patrol leader in the scouts, and had a memorable time in the blitz helping out in hospitals, and erecting Anderson shelters for old people. He attended Battersea Central School till the age of 14 when he left to become an apprentice electrician to a firm in Chelsea. He remembers earning 4¾d per hour! He qualified the hard way, by attending night school, and later gained a place at Battersea Polytechnic, now Westminster College.

Ted remembers the war being a very grey time, with rationing, bombing worries and no men around between the ages of 18 and 40. But on the other hand, people were thrown together: they helped each other, there was less materialism, they talked, played cards, they had to make their own entertainment, there was no television.

After the war, now in his late teens, Ted's social life continued to revolve around the church with dances and scout activities. In 1947 he began his two year's national service in the army, joining the Royal West Kent Infantry for his initial two months training. Then, typical of the army, as a trained electical engineer Ted was assigned to the medical corp, spending 20 weeks being trained at the army school of tropical medicine. He played football and cricket in the army and even boxed. For his last six months while stationed at Mytchett, Ash Vale, he met up with Arthur Bruce and Jock Rutherford the Scottish international footballer and did a great deal of running. Ted and Arthur used to travel back to Battersea together on leave.

Ted came out of the army in 1949 and rejoined the same electrical firm for a short time but soon decided to "do the knowledge" and join his father in the taxi business. He continued to play football and cricket and trained in Battersea Park. When he moved to Ursula Street he lived next door but one to Dennis Jones and his father Bill Jones. They were two life long Belgrave members, and it was they who persuaded Ted to join the club in February 1957.

All through the fifties and sixties Ted was very much part of the club athletics scene competing on the road, country and track. Never one of the stars, nevertheless, Ted enjoyed his athletics particularly the pack runs from Belgrave Hall with the likes of Gerry North, and battles with Charlie Henn in the "5¾". He also got involved with the social life of the club, became Social Secretary and organising the club dinner for five years in the late 'fifties and early 'sixties. They used to be held at the Dog & Fox in Wimbledon. In fact Ted has only missed two club dinners in the whole time he has been a member. He served on the committee undertaking various roles until 1965.

After moving to Woking in the late 60's, life became rather hard for Ted, running his own taxi business and bringing up his two sons. But over the years he never lost touch and has been able to slowly give more time to the club again. Today, although he has bouts of ill health and is troubled by arthritis, he still manages to do ten minutes exercise each day and keep fit by swimming.

Ted does not like to dwell on life's problems, we all have them, but he feels belonging to Belgrave over the years has kept him active both mentally and physically and has helped him through the difficult times.

Since retiring in 1990, Ted has returned wholeheartedly to committee work managing to find time to chair the Membership Services committee as well as organise the club kit. He has also served on the SEAA committee and after travelling to Africa managed to help forge links with several African Athletic organisations such as the Gambian A.A.A. and the Soweto A.A.A. This has involved organising competitive trips and receptions for visiting clubs from these countries.

So Ted comes to the Presidency with a wider perspective than most. Having never been a top athlete or a sectional secretary he has no axe to grind and would very much like to use his influence to spread harmony throughout the club. He recognises that clubs such as Belgrave have to change with the times and people have to accept that, and not harp back to the good old days. At the same time he feels it is vital that the club retains the sportsmanlike spirit of the past.

September 21st 1963 and Ted strides out in the Polytechnic Road Relay at Chiswick.

Left: Nearly two miles gone in the '5¾'. It's September 1966 and among the 39 competitors in the race, this bunch are out to settle personal scores - or perhaps just enjoy the sunshine. The runners are: Financial wizards Keith Miller (106) and Jim Heathfield (93), Ted Pallant (94), talented youth and junior Tony Brooks (37) - lost to the sport before making impact as a senior, Dave McMillan (12) - now fighting back from terrible injuries sustained while out training and hit by a car, P.Blair-Fish (46).

Ted PallantCharlie DickinsonW.E.N. BreachKeith MillerJim HeathfieldTony BrooksDave McMillanP. Blair-FishArthur BruceJock RutherfordDennis JonesBill JonesGerry NorthCharlie Henn

Photograph · page 7

For years Derek Clack has said that in all the time he's been in the club he's not yet had his picture published. Sorry Derek - it's a case of too few photographers required to be in too many places at the right time. However, The Wimbledon Guardian seems to be a little better off in the way of human resource and sent their photographer to snap Derek and Sue after their gruelling hike from John O'Groats to Lands End. The 920 mile walk took our intrepid couple seven weeks but it was well worth it for they raised £1000 for the Tadworth Children's Trust. They got lost for a while at Loch Ness and nearly went over a cliff, got stranded out on the West Highland Way as night fell, suffered from blisters ... makes those training reps up Wool Road seem like a piece of cake!

Derek ClackSue

Column · page 7

Sidetracks

Clive Shippen saw Ernest Obeng amongst the TV crews at the World Junior Championships in Sydney but couldn't get close enough to speak with him. And of course Sir Eddie Kulukundis was a fixture to his seat throughout the Championships. Clive did manage to chat to Mark Shearman a couple of times and mentioned that we'd like a copy of a photo of Tom for The Belgravian. (See front page.)

Young Belgravians tried something a little different on June 16th in the Cinque Ports Triathlon. Alice Ragouzaridis competed in the under 10 event - Swim 100m, Bike 2000m, Run 800m and was a clear winner from a field of 17 boys and girls in 15:17. Paul Graham went in the under 12s where the distances were 150/3000m/1200m. He was way down after the first couple of disciplines, racing his old bike against some hot machines but ripped through the 32 strong field in the 800 metre run before running out of track to finish 9th in 23:19. 15th was Brendon Jackson 24:33. Competing in the under 14 age group but actually just 12 years old, Aaron Jackson placed 25th out of 36 in his 200m/6000m/2000m event.

Winn Shepherd writes to let us know how grateful she is to Snowy Brooks, Ray James and Ray Wilson for their assistance in helping her on and off the coaches taking our teams to their away fixtures and to Sue and Harry Porter, Jackie Stone, Gail Porter and Pat and Alan Mead for their help in providing transport - and of course Jean Hutcherson her main support. It's a pleasure Winn, and while we are at it, many thanks to you for your continued support and help in the running of our home matches.

We hear from Derek Jones that it is extremely likely that he will have to undergo a spell in hospital followed by some extensive convalescence before too long. Derek had lined himself up to be Field Event Referee for many of our forthcoming home fixtures but in typical Belgrave spirit he says, " ... well I won't be able to do that but I will be able to do the announcing from the caravan ..." Well Derek, if you say so and promise not to overdo it. In the meantime all Belgravians will want to wish him well and a swift return to good health.

We also understand that Gwen Couzens is undergoing a prolonged course of medical treatment at the moment. Gwen, we are all thinking of you and look forward to seeing you back in the pink again.

Kassa Tadesse has been sorely missed this winter by our cross country team. After a period of knee and foot trouble he got back to some great road running in the Autumn only for his trouble to flare up again. Latest news is that he is back in training, looking for races and gearing himself for a return to top class running.

More sidetracks

More Sidetracks

An interesting lecture was held at Belgrave Hall back in October. The subject was "Over use in the Adolescent" and the speaker Debbie Annett, who runs a local sports injury therapy service. It was a most interesting subject and we were reminded that young athletes are not mini adults and that there are many, many things to bear in mind when planning sports training for youngsters. It was a pity that more of our coaches could not attend, while coaches from other clubs were totally absent. Perhaps we can repeat the exercise some time.

The thirty-fourth Belgrave Darts Championship was held as normal on the first Saturday evening in December. Again, as normal, Bill Couzens won it. That makes it the last five times in a row to go with the first three times the event was held in 1963-65 plus '69, '71 and '76. One thing that wasn't normal though, we had an all-girl semi-final and Gail Porter was playing with such assurance throughout the competition that it did seem possible that we might see our first ever female champion. But Bill is cool under pressure and he was ever in charge.

Always looked forward to is a letter from our Past President Gordon Doubleday. Gordon tells us that he is keeping fairly fit although cutting back a little on his commitments. He helped timekeep at the Chippenham to Calne Walk and also helped at a Sprint Triathlon earlier in the year. Among social functions he has attended was the Club Dinner of his local athletic club, Somer AC. It was held at the Centurion Hotel, an apt venue for someone who during his athletic career earned himself the coveted Centurion Badge.

Our Youth in Action Scheme has been applauded by Mr. Iain Sproat, MP, the Minister for Sport, Department of National Heritage. At a special awards ceremony held in 1996 where schemes from all across the Nation were taken into consideration, we were only beaten into second place by a very grandiose scheme being run by Warwickshire County Cricket Club. Well done to everyone involved in running this scheme.

The New Orleans Jazz Night at the Hall in December was a great success as was the Burns Night Supper on 25th January. At the latter event we were again entertained by the Scottish Folk duo known as Ptarmigan, and with a piper, a kilted Lionel Mann overseeing cooking of the Haggis and MC-ing the evening plus some entertainment from our own members a great night was had by all.

Eric Hall was at the British Museum Newspaper library some while back, and delving into the "Sporting Clipper" for May 11th 1889 noted that at the Polytechnic H & CC Meeting on 4th May 1889 at Paddington Recreation Ground - J. Gilbert of Belgrave Harriers was third in a mile handicap from a field of 138 out of 165 entries. What a nightmare for the track judges that must have been. A report in the same paper but dated 9th November 1889 on a meeting held seven days earlier at Tufnell Park by Spartan Harriers mentions that the four mile junior inter-club race was won by Beaumont H with Falcon H 2nd and Belgrave H 3rd. The newspaper mentions that athletic clubs were springing up all over the country. Eric notes that some sprung and stayed while others faded or merged. Of the early names mentioned he recognised Poly, SLH, London AC, Finchley and Blackheath but even he is too young to remember Reindeer, Beaumont or Falcon!

The determined Dave McMillan continues to slowly improve after his awful accident early in 1996 when he was hit by a car while out training for the London Marathon. He has had great set-backs but is now out of his wheelchair, he attended the Club Dinner in November, and on New Year's Eve threw away his crutches. Dave continues to have severe problems with his arm and is full of pieces of metal holding him together but don't rule out seeing him in the Yacht '3' again one day - even if he has to strap his arm to his chest!

Tough guys these marathon runners. George Piddington is now living in his own flat down in Hampshire and his local club Havant AC are planning something special for him. George never did do his 50 marathons but Havant are planning to push him in his wheelchair on Sunday 27th April 1997 in the South Coast Marathon at Gosport. Are there any of George's old Belgrave mates who would like to take part and help George to another marathon? If so contact Peter Spencer, telephone [phone number withheld]. Incidentally, as a coach at Havant for many years now, George must have been delighted when his team won the Southern Women's Cross Country Champs at Parliament Hill.

Obituary · page 8

Farewell to old comrades

Gordon Doubleday

We are sorry to learn of the deaths of several of our very long standing members. Each in his own way made a valuable contribution to the Club. We will remember them for their sportsmanship and companionship and will mourn their passing.

Bill Merryman was born in 1916 and joined us in October 1931 as a runner. For years he trained at Wimbledon and Battersea Park. He loved taking part in "mob" matches where everyone counted. When the days came for Club Championships and Track Matches he would turn up as an ardent supporter. When World War 2 (1939-45) came he was able to continue his running with the R.A.F. Always ready to lend a hand he helped at Track Meetings, particularly the Field Events, a job he continued to do long after his active running days were over. His enthusiasm for the Club was carried over to the social side and he was a regular attender at these functions. In recognition for what he did for the Club he was elected President for 1979/80. He eventually left London to live at Newhaven (Sussex) but his interest in the Club and its fortunes never faded.

Fred Denny was born in 1930 and joined us in 1955. A powerfully built policeman he turned out regularly in Club and Open walks and, of course, for the "boys in blue" in their Championships and matches. A keen student of the mechanics of race-walking he took up judging after ceasing to be active. His prowess in this field saw him rise to the grade of Chief Judge. In addition to showing the Red Card (dq.) he would have a chat with the "offender" telling him/her of the fault in their style and how to correct it. He was elected President of the R.W.A. in 1993/4 and during his term he made an appeal for funds to assist the development of young walkers. He recently had a heart attack from which he was recovering when he took a holiday abroad. On the journey home he had another attack which proved fatal.

Sadly we also recently received the news of the death of A.H. ("Ben") Knifton. His is a name few will remember and few current members will have known him in spite of the fact that he had been a Belgravian for seventy-one years and was a Vice-President of the Club. Born in 1907 he joined us in 1925; he was a strong and steady walker. During his career there was only one National Senior Championship, the 20 miles, and in 1929 at Hayes (Kent) he scored in our winning team. As part of his training he would often do an all day stroll in the country at week-ends. This gave him good leg strength and stamina, which, combined with speed work with Club "packs" made him a formidable competitor. Some may remember his son, John, who walked for us and emigrated to the U.S.A.where he reached the top ranks of walkers there. Several years ago Ben left the country to live at the sunny Algarve (Portugal). Knowing him he probably found some grand walks in that area.

Lovell Cole has also passed on. He was 85 and in his 58th year of membership.

So we say "farewell" to these stalwarts and think of their friendship and of the parts they played in the life and fortunes of the Club.

Gordon Doubleday

Gordon DoubledayBill MerrymanFred DennyA.H. (Ben) KniftonJohn KniftonLovell Cole

Report · page 9

Youth in Action 96

THE BELGRAVE Youth In Action Presentation Evening on December 6th was a highly successful affair. Last year it was held at Belgrave Hall but our HQ was bursting at the seams as we tried to squeeze getting on for 200 people into our function room. This time we tried something different having been offered the use of The Polka Theatre at the end of Wimbledon Broadway. It was just the job.

Many of our young athletes and their parents came along and we welcomed special guests in the personages of Sir Eddie Kulukundis OBE (our Club Patron), John Hadley (Representative of The Sports Council), Mike Da Silva (Chairman of the National Young Athletes League) and of course our Club President Ted Pallant.

A glittering array of bronze, silver and gold-ware was handed over to our young athletes, together with certificates and trophies ... so many that one had to keep reminding one's-self of the incredibly high standards at which these youngsters compete: 15 year-old 2 metre high-jumpers, 58 metre hammer throwers, 10.62 sprinters, 14 year-old 6.42m long jumpers ... it just went on and on.

Having exceeded our four year targets in half that time one wonders just where it will end. Our Junior Men have been promoted to Division One, our Young Athletes placed 3rd in their Premier Division and went to the National Finals ... the only area where we have not achieved unqualified success is in being unable to break into the Girls' National YA League. Here, with YA matches scheduled for the day after Southern Women's Leagues, where events are also included for youngsters, we were unable to turn out more than half a dozen at a time - in matches where we really need about fifty athletes.

Well done John Powell for organising the evening.

Report · page 10

Bels down under ...

Eric Hall

Our roving reporter Eric Hall checks out the facilities for Sydney 2000, meets up with a few expatriates ... and relives Melbourne '56

Recent issues of "The Belgravian" have made mention of the Belgrave interests in Australia and with the run up to the Sydney Olympics, only three years and a bit away, more will occur. It was with this in mind that this reporter snatched at the Editor's invitation to go out to see for himself just what was happening! No expense spared I was told and so Mavis came as navigator, interpreter and general factotum.

The starting point was Sydney and we were soon made aware of the importance of the year 2000 to the residents. Only one told us that they planned to exit the city for September when the Games are to be held! The reason was that accommodation will be at a premium and air flights out of Australia very cheap at that time so this young lady was all for making a killing. Everyone else seemed besotted by sport and determined to show the world just what could be done with an Olympic Games.

We toured the facilities available at the moment and saw the hole in the ground which is to be the main stadium. As ever there is talk of the building unions applying pressure to the organisers in order to hike their wages! The Aquatic Centre, as with all facilities we saw during our trip, is very impressive - larger than ever and with plenty of training facilities alongside. Within the precincts of the Homebush area we watched a road walk in progress but were unable to get to chat with the officials.

On our final day in the city we were entertained by Clive and Elizabeth Shippen. Not wishing to be overlooked in the crowd, and it being a blustery day, I wore a Belgrave sweater and Clive responded by the rather more essential Belgrave cap. (By the way Ted, Clive is still waiting for the three he ordered months ago!) A tour of the beaches north of Sydney and the NSW track and training area, was followed by an inspection of the Shippen's new abode. Just one word of warning for anyone challenged to a spin by Clive - make sure it is on the flat as his drive rises at 45 degrees from the road and I am sure he intends to use it for interval training! They both wish to be remembered to everyone in the Club and I know Clive would relish the Surrey Cross Country performance over his old home course of Coulsdon. First individual and team prize is a real tonic to everyone.

We moved on to Canberra and I aired my Club kit on the Institute of Sport track. Whether it was my style, speed or elegance that caused the assembled group of budding internationals to stop and stare I don't know but I was delighted to hear a shout of "Come on Belgrave". Canberra has everything needed by an ambitious athlete. We were left wondering why Australia doesn't win all the gold medals?

Belgrave Station, just outside Melbourne, has featured in this magazine before but we couldn't resist the photo opportunity by draping the club vest across the station bench - quite what the Japanese tourists made of it we don't know - especially as it was raining at the time. We then boarded the Puffing Billy steam train for a beautiful ride through the countryside at a speed much the same as that I used to manage up Copse Hill - I probably did rather more puffing!

Melbourne will always have a special place in my heart and yet again - after 40 years - it turned up trumps. Apart from the Editor's kind invitation to tour Australia the motive behind our visit was to attend the 40th anniversary celebrations of the Olympic Games of 1956. This included a tour of the Village at Heidelberg and a dinner, attended by over a thousand, across the road from the Melbourne Cricket Ground (MCG). We were prevented from using the MCG as it was needed for a concert by an M. Jackson. We met many old sparring partners, Ray Smith, Ron Crawford and Noel Freeman to say nothing of chatting late into the night with John Landy, Merv Lincoln and Ron Clark. We finally got away about 1.45 am and wandered along the Yarra to find a taxi back to the hotel. A very emotional evening finished up by the taxi driver announcing that a countryman of his had won a gold medal in 1956 - who else but the late Norman Read who had been so much in our minds during our stay.

A few days on the road, amid outstanding scenery, found us in Adelaide where we stayed with Terry Clark - Belgrave's national junior champion in 1958. Again he passes his regards to all who remember him. He made sure we had access to all manner of sporting venues and people responsible for spotting talent and developing it whether it be cricket, athletics, cycling or hockey. All were enthusiasts and took time off from whatever they were doing to explain the Australian system and philosophy to us.

Somewhere along the line we had time to surf the Internet, with Terry's aid, and found a list of Olympic medallists. Browsing through this we came up with a real Belgrave surprise. It seems that the bronze medal in the 1960 20km walk was won by a Stanley F Vickers (no problem with that) but the nationality given was Germany! No wonder conversation was always a problem!

Our final evening was spent at an Italian restaurant where the proprietor collects autographed photographs of his sporting clients. My publicity managers not only found an old photograph of yours truly but I was prevailed upon (in an inebriated state) to sign it and when last seen it was being placed on the wall by the side of one signed by a Donald Bradman but I am not sure what event he was in!

Photo: Mavis Hall

Two Bels. on a beach. Clive Shippen (left) and Eric Hall meet up on a North Sydney shoreline.

Notice · page 10

263 Club Records

Two issues ago you were no doubt astounded to read that 115 Club Records had been broken during the nine months up to September 30th 1995. Well, your statisticians have recently fought their way from beneath a mountain of result sheets and have now come up with the even more startling news that since that date and up to the time of writing at December 1st 1996, a period of 14 months, no fewer than 263 Club Records have been equalled or beaten. Takes some believing doesn't it! We had promised to publish a list in the Belgravian showing each advance in our best ever marks - but quite honestly we just cannot do so - it would take five full pages of this magazine. We do, however, present a reprint of our younger age group walking records, almost totally rewritten during the last year. These can be found on pages 46-47.

Obituary · page 10

"HW's" Barry Jones passes away

We were very sorry to learn that Hercules Wimbledon A.C.'s General Hon. Secretary Barry Jones had died suddenly in January. Barry gave a great deal of his life to the sport of athletics and didn't just confine his efforts to his club. If there was an athletic meeting being promoted virtually anywhere in South London, Barry was sure to be found on the time-keepers' stand and athletics in Surrey will be very much the poorer for his passing. Our commiserations go to our friends at "HW".

Barry Jones

Report · page 11

Belgravians help improve National Walking Standards

Stephen Whorlow

Race Walking

The Race Walking scene in our Club is taking us up the rankings and we are now one of the Nation's Top Clubs in the Young Athletes sections with many successes coming our way. We are still missing out in the Senior's age group with only one of our walkers filling that gap but our Veterans are still reasonably strong and active. With luck this Senior gap will be filled within the next three or four years by our up and coming youngsters.

The problem has been that it is almost impossible to introduce new blood into an age group (Seniors) that has hardly any representation. But with the growth of Race Walking again nationally, especially in the Junior sections, we shall hopefully overcome this problem. The Junior numbers are growing and this was noticed at the AAA Championships in Birmingham this year when there had to be a dual start in two of the events to enable the races to be accomodated on the track. Race Walking had some of the largest individual entries in these Championships and the RWA Road Championships held in Folkestone had the highest number of entries for many years (and remember they were held in what used to be a barren area for Walking). This has done away with the old idea that Race Walking Medals are easily won. Not any more, they are hard fought for, and against some very good competition.

Mabon Dane has been our only Senior and has flown the flag all round the country. This year he has represented the South and Essex whilst among the Juniors Andrew Goudie become a fully fledged Belgravian and gained our first full Junior England Vest for a long time, along with his Southern, Kent County and London Schools representative honours and being Ranked number three in his age group.

New to Walking is Mick Graham, one of the Club's Cross Country and Road Running stalwarts. Mick is already knocking on the door of future Veteran Representation and he says that he finds the friendship around the country in the Walking scene very refreshing. So why not try the event, those in the Walking Section will make you most welcome!

In the Under 17 section Nigel Whorlow has continued to progress through the rankings, finishing in the top 7 on both Track and Road in the Under 20 section. He, along with Laura Fryer gained their RWA "Young England " Vests, as well as SEAA, Kent County and Kent Schools Vests. Both were also 3rd. in their respective National Road Championships. The Under 13 Age Group brought us the surprise of the year in young Paul Graham who has taken to Walking as well as his Cross-Country and Middle Distance track competitions. He is ranked at the top of his age group. The Veterans have also had their say in the walking scene. Dave Sharpe competing well in the World Vets. Championships and for his County, also being the first walker to complete 100 races in the LPR Blackheath Handicap series. John Hall also represented his County (Middlesex) during the summer.

Overall the Walking scene has brought back to the Club numerous Championship Medals and Trophies. The most prized of all was the presentation of the Sparta Trophy for being the most Improved Club overall in the country during 1995. (The trophy not being presented until May this year.) We won many County, Regional and National Championships and gained places on both Road and Track in all age groups, along with County and National Schools Championships, giving us three international representatives, an ESAA Champion, 5 SEAA and 6 County Champions).

When you note the size of our small team you will realise that this has been a great effort within only two seasons of walking being re-introduced into these age groups in the Club.

We have also, because of our youngsters' success and with help of our friends in Dartford Harriers, managed to get Race Walking re-introduced into the Schools County Championships. This in turn has helped get the event going in various schools around Kent, which can only be good for the sport and the Club in the long run (or Walk!).

One of the nice surprises of the year was the receipt by our "Miniature Walking Dynamite" Lucy Asling, of a book from the USA in which she has been used as an example of the perfect walking style. Not bad to get International recognition at the age of nine. Lucy had finished the 1995/96 year having beaten all comers at 1km. (and most of the boys as well) on the Track and Road.

The Young Athletes Grand Prix season was exceptionally long, with 11 events, but we still managed to get competitors to all the races, with two competing at every one. As can been seen elsewhere in this "Belgravian" we finished very high up most of the Age group sections and the Club finished one place higher overall (4th) than in 1995.

Having taken on the re-introduced Club Race Walking Secretary's job I am finding the task something of a challenge, both on the financial side, and with the many other aspects of the job. My main aim will be to get the Walking Section well represented at all levels and make sure that everyone who wishes to compete can find the event that will suit them; then make sure that they can get to the venue to compete.

Finally may I wish all our Walkers established and new, young and well trod, a prosperous and successful year, and let's see if we can get more Teams out during 1997.

Stephen Whorlow

In the N.U.T.S. 1996 Rankings the Race Walking Section of the Club has faired as well, if not better than many of the other Young and Junior Athletes Groups within the Club. Whilst some may say that there are not as many Race Walkers as there are competitors in other events, you still have to be above the N.U.T.S. Standard to figure in their Rankings, as in all events. We managed the following Track Track Rankings -

Under 13 Girls (2,000m.) - Laura Fryer 2nd. Under 15 Girls (3,000m.) - Laura Fryer(U13) 10th. Under 17 Ladies (3,000m.) - Kelly-Anne Parker-Smith 4th. Under 15 Boys (3,000m.) - Christopher Stanford 6th. Under 17 Men (5,000m.) - Nigel Whorlow 4th. Under 20 Men (10,000m.) - Andrew Goudie 3rd.

On the Road we faired just as well, and from my own Rankings list, taken from published results they were-

Results · page 12

RWA YAG Grand Prix Series

The matches in this series were held at Brierly Hill, Victoria Park London, Crystal Palace, Coventry, Weymouth, Chesterfield, Folkestone, Steyning, Birmingham, Tamworth and Enfield. Only two athletes completed in all eleven events and both were from Belgrave Harriers: Laura Fryer (U13G) and Nigel Whorlow (U17M). Of the eleven events the best six for each athlete went towards their final score with points awarded as follows: 1st = 40, 2nd = 38, 3rd = 37, and so on down to 40th. Any finisher not in the first 40 scored one point.

Report · page 12

Cambridge H League Match 1, 19 Oct 1996

In spite of the team being depleted because of the National Relays taking place on the same day in Chesterfield the overall results were better than expected.

In the under 11 events we took first in both the Girls' and the Boys' events. Lucy Asling (the country's top Under 11) won the Girls' events and Sam McNalley the Boys'. In the Under 15 race Sophie Read was our only other Girl competing and she took third spot.

The Seniors fared little better in numbers with Dave Sharpe taking 6th place.

Lucy AslingSam McNalleySophie ReadDave Sharpe

Report · page 12

RWA Relays Chesterfield, 19 Oct 1996

Belgrave entered one female and two male teams in this event and had high hopes of bringing back the event silverware, but as the saying goes, "The best laid plans of Mice and Men etc." It did not work out as well as we hoped. Team selection let us down in the end and even though all were very competitive it was over excitement, as in many relay events, that let us down. There were too many Penalty Cards!

In the Female 16km event we came eighth, with Laura Fryer (2km), Kelly-Anne Parker-Smith (6km), Victoria Parker-Smith (4km) and Sue Porter (6km) making up the team.

The Male 22km. event gave us a little more success with Danny Bond (2km), Nigel Whorlow (4km), Mabon Dane (6km), Andrew Goudie (4km) and John Hall (6km) making up the 'A' Team. The team took the Bronze Medal, to add to their 1995 Silver but should have been the Relay Champions. As previously mentioned the Team gained far too many Penalty Cards which pushed us out of first place.

The 'B' Team ended up incomplete with two of its selected members unable to make the event, so Nigel Whorlow and Mabon Dane filled in to allow the other team members to compete, even though it was a non-scoring team. The team consisted of Stephen Whorlow Jnr (2km), Danny Bond (4km), Carl Lawton (6km), Chris Stanford (4km), Nigel Whorlow (4km) and Mabon Dane (6km), and they came home eighth.

Women's 16km 4 Stage Relay (2k, 4k, 6k, 4k) 1, Sheffield 1:20:28; 8, Belgrave 1:43.17. Men's 22km 6 Stage Relay (2k, 4k, 6k, 4k, 6k) 1, Leicester 1:49:46; 2, Coventry 1:51:19; 3, Belgrave 1:54:43 (C.Stanford 10:28, N.Whorlow 19:30, M.Dane 30:03, A.Goudie 18:56, J.Hall 28:48, +7.00 penalties); 8, Belgrave 'B' 2:11:47.

Belgrave had seven selected athletes at this match and another who was placed second. Results · page 12

English Schools Race Walking Champs Birmingham, 21 Sep 1996

Track Belgrave Harriers took more than half of the Kent Schools Race Walking Team to these Championships. Out of a total of fourteen, we supplied eight athletes and the Club also provided the winner of the Senior Boys event with Andrew Goudie representing London Schools; nine Belgrave athletes in all. To add to Andrew's gold, Nigel Whorlow overcame a bad middle section of of his race to take the Intermediates' bronze medal (and the ESAA Standard Badge to add to his 1995 ESAA Junior Boys' silver.

Junior Boy Chris Stanford just missed out on a Bronze after a last lap tussle with the King twins from Essex, but he did manage to gain his ESAA Standard Badge, and Top Six Certificate.

Report · page 13

Bradford YA Grand Prix 9 Nov 1996: The Longest Day

An early start to what is possibly the longest trip (18 hours) the team will make during the coming Grand Prix League Season was made on a cold and wet Saturday morning. Nine trains and two bus journeys were the order of the day.

Because of the distance and other commitments the team was depleted but we still had 7 Young Athletes and one Senior competing and we came home with a trophy for each that completed the event.

The Under 11 Girls 1km gave us a third place to new member Emma McNalley, whilst in the Under 13 Boys event her brother Sam took his first Grand Prix Title by winning the 2km with a personal best. With no Under 15 Boys competing we relied on Laura Fryer in this age group. Laura finished the 1995/96 season Nationally ranked no. 2, and she confirmed her status as one of the leading Under 15 Girls by bringing home the 'goodies' in her 3km race by placing third.

More was to come in the Youths 5km event where Nigel Whorlow confirmed his top National ranking in his second year in the age group, taking his second consecutive Grand Prix win and making himself favourite for the Grand Prix Title at the end of next summer. In third place was fast improving Chris Stanford gaining a massive personal best. Unfortunately Danny Bond had a bad day and had to withdraw after 3km while Robert Matelle never got started after gashing his leg while running to the start. He required 10 stitches for his troubles.

To add to the successes of day, Mick Graham (M45) gained a 7th place in the Seniors 10km and is fast becoming a force in the Veteran Races around the country.

These results have put the Club into a very secure second place in the overall team category of the Grand Prix National League.

Under 11 Girls' 1km: 3, E.McNalley 7:42. Under 13 Boys' 1km: 1, S.McNalley 10:56. Under 15 Girls' 3km: 3, L.Fryer 17:03. Under 17 Men's 5km: 1, N.Whorlow 24:06; 3, C.Stanford 26:13. Senior Men's 10km: 7, M.Graham 55:47.

Emma McNalleySam McNalleyLaura FryerNigel WhorlowChris StanfordDanny BondRobert MatelleMick Graham

Feature · page 13

Belgrave dominate season's debut event

No not a headline taken from the South London Press, not even Athletics Weekly - but the Colchester Evening Gazette! It's not the only East Anglian paper to pick up on Belgrave's activities in their area either, for the East Anglian Daily Times also recently devoted three columns of its sports pages to the phenomena of Race Walking taking off in the Suffolk area in spite of the lack of local facilities. Even the local TV station has featured our young Belgravians going through their paces during training sessions.

Mick Graham has been taking part in our cross country and road races for years. With his son Paul showing an early aptitude for all sports but in particular those of running and walking, it was natural that Belgrave should soon take on another recruit. Then Philip and Andrew quickly followed the course taken by their older brother and in turn schoolfriends at Chalkestone Middle School were impressed by the success that came the way of our young members. Before long West Suffolk had turned into a little hotbed of Belgrave activity. With top walker Mabon Dane on hand to advise and coach, and Dave Sharpe throwing in his expertise, Mick Graham himself was soon encouraged to have a go and now it is not at all unusual to see the claret and gold dominating the Colchester Track Handicaps and winning medals in all age groups from Veterans down to Under 11s.

Mick GrahamPaul GrahamPhilip GrahamAndrew GrahamMabon DaneDave Sharpe

Report · page 13

Midland RWA Winter League Match 2 Solihull, 16 Nov 1996

On a good day for walking and on a course that was fast enough to pull out early season PBs, the Club only had 3 walkers out. This did not hinder Under 17 Nigel Whorlow who improved his 10km. time by exactly a minute, although he had hoped for a 'sub-50', and he came home the leading Under 17 and 3rd Under 20. His actual race position of 8th in a field full Seniors with over 30 finishing was excellent. Danny Bond, competing in only his third 5km race since moving up an age group in October, found the the course hard going. He nearly pulled out after 3km but managed to continue on to finish and add to his league score ready for the final match to follow in December.

Senior Men's 10km: 8, N.Whorlow (U17M) 51:15; 17, D.Fotheringham 56:39. Under 17 Men's 5km: 3, D.Bond 30:25. Under 13 Boys' 2km: 3, S.McNally 11:59.

Report · page 13

Midland RWA Winter League Match 3 Tamworth, 1 Dec 1996

This match was originally scheduled to take place at Draycot Water, Rugby but because of last minute problems it was moved to Tamworth, to be held over the same course as used for Match 1.

This caused travel problems for our team of four but thanks to the Midland's Walking fraternity we managed to get lifts from Birmingham to the course.

Nigel Whorlow, who had already taken over a minute and a half off his best during the previous two matches went out to break the 50 minute barrier but after only 3km suffered hip problems and had to pull out. Danny Bond, being the only Bels walker left to complete all three races came home 4th and took the Overall 3rd place in the Handicap section of the league. Chris Stanford, who missed the second match, came in 3rd. Laura Fryer took part in the U15 Girls race and managed to beat some of her new rivals in her new age group, coming home third.

Also travelling over for the match were four of our Suffolk youngsters. Emma McNally surprised everyone by coming home first in the U11 Girls' event and another pleasant surprise was young Andrew Graham who took second place in the U11 Boys race. Not to be out done by their younger brothers and sisters, Paul Graham beat fancied favourite Luke Finch in the U13 Boys taking around two minutes off the Club record, with his team-mate Sam McNalley filling third spot.

The Midland League, which was undertaken as a competitive training exercise, produced three PBs for a League medal.

Under 17 Men's 5km: 3, C. Standford 29:29; 4, D.Bond 29:47. Under 15 Girls' 3km: 3, L.Fryer 17:35. Under 13 Boys' 2km: 1, P.Graham 10:27; 3, S.McNally 11:32. Under 11 Boys' 1km: 2, A.Graham 6:13. Under 11 Girls' 1km: 1, E.Mcnally 6:26.

Nigel WhorlowDanny BondChris StanfordLaura FryerEmma McNallyAndrew GrahamPaul GrahamLuke FinchSam McNalley

Notice · page 13

Ancient Club Bests begin to tumble

The Walkers have been extremely active on the Club Record breaking scene. Of the Seniors Mabon Dane seemed to race everywhere but he has some of the greatest walking marks that Britain has seen to aim at. In the younger age-groups our ancient club bests did begin to tumble, however, as Andrew Goudie, Laura Fryer, Nigel Whorlow and others reminded the athletics world of the name of that famous old walking club - Belgrave Harriers.

Mabon DaneAndrew GoudieLaura FryerNigel Whorlow

Report · page 15

Lerwill wins silver in World Champs.

Track & Field

Clive Shippen attended each day of the World Junior Championships in Sydney.

I was very impressed: one could almost see him maturing during the championships themselves. In the first round he finished a comfortable second to 'British' rival James Nolan of Ireland whose pb was 1.38 secs faster than Tom's 1:49.18. In the second round he really turned on the pace; he had to, because he was up against Kenya's Japhet Kimutai (a name to watch) who stormed to a 1:45.63 win. But Tom chased him home with great fervour to clock a brilliant pb of 1:47.27 - a huge cut off his previous best. Nolan was behind him this time in 4th.

What a joy it was on day three to see a Briton in the final of the 800 metres; such a change these days. And what a change it was to see that man get a medal, and especially the way he won it. The formidable Kenyan took the field through 200m in 24.93 and I was relieved to see Tom show restraint and slip into a trouble-free 7th. At the bell (53.34) he moved out and went into 6th, and down the back straight ran with power to reach 4th at 600 (1:21.12). Round the final bend he was nicely positioned and went to 3rd entering the straight. It was another Kenyan Mwengi Mutua who now moved away to win as the favourite Kimutai faded to 4th but Tom dug deep, kept his form beautifully and to the chagrin of the home crowd eased past Australian Grant Cremer to secure the silver medal.

His time of 1:48.40 did not match that lightning fast semi but it was the way that he ran the race that impressed me. Many Juniors are impetuous at this level: they get caught up in the intensity and passion of the occasion. But Tom looked as cool as a cucumber as he ran his own first 300 metres. I also liked the strength of his running (lacking from the present crop of British runners) and his tactical sense. It was an outstanding performance and Belgrave have good cause to be proud of him.

Result: 1, M.Mutua (Kenya) 1:48.21; 2, T.Lerwill (GB) 1:48.40; 3, G.Cremer (Australia) 1:48.46.

Tom's efforts did not finish there as he was second runner in the 4x400 metres team. I was not too optimistic as none of the British 400m and 400m hurdles runners had reached their finals. However, they chased the Americans hard to finish second in their heat and nearly did the same in the final, except that Japan snatched the silver. What a tradition Britain has in this event: everyone seems to run above themselves. Again Tom was impressive. He took that awkward second leg where no one seems to know where they are until the break occurs on the back straight. Two runners shot past him but again he showed admirable control, overtook them both and finished very strongly to hand over in 3rd place which the team held to the finish. So he ran five quality races in five days. Watch out Kenyans.

Clive ShippenTom LerwillJames NolanJaphet KimutaiMwengi MutuaGrant Cremer

Belgrave hurdlers earn Jarrett his points

Haringey's international hurdler Tony Jarrett was guest competitor on BBC's Question of Sport recently but he was forced to utter the names of Belgrave hurdlers to earn his points on the programme. In the video footage the name of the A.A.A. 110m Hurdles Champion for 1994 was required: answer - Andy Tulloch. And then for his home and away question "Who won a A.A.A. hurdle race 9 years after winning his first?" Answer - Jon Ridgeon.

Notice · page 15

Larios lose their stars

For some years now the European Clubs T&F Championship has been dominated by the Spanish Club Larios (Larios being Spain's top selling gin). With a huge budget the club were able to set up a deal whereby a great majority of Cuba's international team were able to perform in the Spanish club's colours. However, Adidas Running Team have now signed up the Cuban stars and for good measure have taken on board a couple of top Spaniards as well. It will be interesting to see if Larios now stay in the sport - if they don't then the European Clubs event becomes just that little bit more open.

Report · page 16

AAA Championships & Olympic Trials, Birmingham 14-16 Jun 1996

The chase for places on Britain's team for Atlanta was on with a vengeance at the Olympic Trials at Birmingham's Alexander Stadium and the event surrounded by the most hype was that 400 metres. Dave Nolan ran 46.48 in his heat and then 46.68 in the semi - but one really needed to go sub-46 to get into the final, such was the high standard. Du'aine Ladejo went out hard, as did the Welshman Iwan Thomas but Roger Black was in the form of his life and coming strongly off the bend he came through for a British Record 44.39 while Ladejo had to be content with 2nd in a personal (and Club) best of 44.66. Thomas and Baulch also ducked well under 45 seconds in this classiest ever British one-lap race.

John Regis had to dig deep and use every ounce of his strength to stay a millimetre or two ahead of Christie to take the 200m - both were given the same time to the hundredth.

Vaulters Edwards and Tullett gained 3rd and 4th places but with Crawley's Buckfield setting a British Best of 5.71m to win, no-one else was going to be Atlanta bound unless they exceeded the qualifying mark. The same sort of fate befell Kevin Brown, 3rd in the Discus as Birchfield's big Bob Weir went over 60m but Hammer thrower Dave Smith confirmed his number one standing this season with another throw over 72 metres.

Paul Evans, already selected for an event he did not want to do in the Olympics - the marathon - gave the selectors the option to slot him into the 10,000m by getting into the frame and Andy Tulloch also booked his ticket in the high hurdles while Neil Owen missed out by 2/100ths of a second and two places.

Justin Chaston fought off the tenacious Keith Cullen to take the steeplechase but the parade event for the Bels was the 400m hurdles. No fewer than three of our club-men had made it through to the final: Rawlinson, Levy and Ridgeon, and this in spite of the fact that they had already all faced each other in the same semi-final. Down the back stretch the claret and gold colours were reduced by a third when Noel Levy, a great talent at only 21 years of age, hit a hurdle badly and took an almighty tumble. Ex-Gladiators contestant Chris Rawlinson held on as well as he could but could not quite match his semi-final time while Jon Ridgeon showed that he was the absolute master of the race. As far as 'Joe Public' was concerned Ridgeon and Black were the stars of the meeting both back at the top in British Athletics after years of injury.

Report · page 16

The 1996 Olympic Games, Atlanta, 26 Jul - 4 Aug 1996

Atlanta Braves

Seven Belgravians made it to the Olympic Games, a tremendous achievement. Justin Chaston, Paul Evans and Andy Tulloch all managed to produce their season's best performances at just the right time - you can't ask much more than that - while Jon Ridgeon was only just outside his best for the year and with good health and a full winter's preparation may well improve yet on his life-time best of 48.73 come the 1997 World Champs. Andy Tulloch's 13.56 was only a shade outside his best ever run and made him the new Belgrave sprint hurdles record holder.

John Regis must have been a bit disappointed. He was unable to get back to the level of the early season form that he had shown in South Africa and for such a be-medalled athlete not to reach the final was galling. Also disappointed was Du'aine Ladejo who looked totally out of sorts and could not get near his 44.66 from the AAA Champs.

Our giant Hammer thrower Dave Smith had his work cut out. Faced with a phalanx of Eastern Europeans who can heave their implement over 75 metres as a matter of course, Dave had to at least replicate his best of 75.10m to stand a chance of reaching the final and to be sure, had to beat the standard of 76.50m. This time around the feat was beyond him but for this young man Atlanta must be seen as a learning experience and future Games are sure to see him become Britain's best ever hammer thrower.

No medals then - but how proud we are to have seen seven of our number compete in the Greatest Show on Earth.

Report · page 17

Match 2, Copthall, 9 Jun 1996: Somehow we salvage 3rd place

British Athletics League Division One

Somehow we salvage 3rd place

We entered this local fixture still reeling from the 8 point defeat handed to us by newly promoted Sale Manchester in Match 1. Liverpool had virtually been a home fixture for them and we wondered how well they would travel away from home.

Despite the loss of big point scorers Dave Nolan, Neil Owen and Chris Rawlinson to an International match in Estonia, we felt confident that we had retained enough fire power to shake the opposition. The feeling of confidence evaporated almost as soon as the match began. The first disaster saw Fred Salle pull up lame in the long jump run up and he retired without a single leap. Soon after Richard Aspden decided to open at 2.05m in in the high jump and failed to record a height. Multi-eventer, Simon Shirley became the 'A' string in both events and despite good performance we were suddenly 40 points down on the team manager's forecast.

Languishing in an unlikely 7th spot up to half way it looked as though we had blown our league chances completely. Strong performances in the 800m and 1500m by Matt Yates in his UK track debut for Belgrave, plus the usual solid performances by all our throws squad brought a semblance of respectability to our score. Maximum points in the vault and solid relay performances saw us creep into 3rd spot - two places ahead of Sale.

In the end we finished just 17½ points behind winners Birchfield, who moved into the lead position as we turned our thoughts to Match 3.

Report · page 17

Alexander Stadium, Birmingham, 6 Jul 1996: And now its all to play for

Bill Laws

And now its all to play for

With Birchfield now heading the league and Match 3 being a home fixture for the Birmingham based Club, we put out a 5 line whip in an effort to get maximum support. Our policy is to get as many of the team into a local hotel the night before to minimise the risk of the 'day trippers' not arriving .

The first event proved a huge success with Atlanta bound Dave Smith and throws Captain Phil Spivey getting maximum points in the hammer. Geoffrey Ojok couldn't get his run up right in the long jump and failed to make the cut, while Birchfield gained a one two with the help of super vet Barrington Williams. This was followed by the first track event, the 400 hurdles, and so depleted were we that Neil Owen ran the 'B' leg. Rumour had it that he had been doing some over distance training which seemed to pay off until he hit the home straight. His 55.12 was a good début but below his expectations however, and he swore - never again.

The start was depressingly familiar but the 800m raised spirits. Tom Lerwill showed tactical awareness and a kick which belied his youth. So effective was he that he held off Olympics bound Kevin McKay for a fine win and a notable scalp. Matt Kloiber showed that he was over his illness problems with a solid 3rd spot in the 'B' race. The short sprint got the experts reaching for their rule books as both Andrew Walcott and Birchfield's Michael Rosswess were disqualified for two false starts. Interesting! But no points. Marlon Dickson ran a PB to finish second in the 'B' race and into a headwind!

Better points in the 3000m steeplechase, excellent results in the discus and a win in the high hurdles from a tired Neil Own saw a revival of fortunes. Ian Wells once again stepped into the 'B' 110 hurdles as it became apparent that our scheduled hurdler was still in London. (How did we get on without mobile phones?) From there on, the only wins were Lee Newman in the 'B' discus, Nigel Bevan in the 'A' javelin and a rather tasty victory in the long relay.

We had been picking up good points in some events and losing them in others. No 'B' runner in the 1500m showed how difficult it was for us to find an uninjured middle distance man able to make the start line. So once again, third place on the day and we remained second in the League with, surprise, surprise, Sale moving back into the lead on 20 league points and three teams close behind on 19.

What should have been a win could so easily have been a disaster and yet the team fought for every point to give us a reasonable chance of success going into the last match - a home fixture to boot!

Belgrave's team managers had the foresight last season to remove their names from the League Register, so couldn't be co-opted into the 'B' 1500!

Report · page 18

Match 4, Kingsmeadow, 17 Aug 1996: Over the moon ... then sick as parrots

Bill Laws

British Athletics League Division One

Over the moon ... then sick as parrots

This was it. A home fixture and the big one. We knew that any one of four teams could still win the league. With our strongest middle distance squad since the heady days of entering the league 14 years ago, we knew we were in with a shout. All we had to do was beat Sale by 12 points and we would be representing the UK in the European Clubs Championship again next year.

The start went to plan. Maximum points in the hammer, a weak 'B' long jump and an excellent high jump with Richard Aspden taking the 'A' event plus an injured 400 hurdler in the form of Chris Rawlinson finishing second in the 'B' high jump. Chris, unable to run his specialist 400 hurdles for much of the season also scored valuable points in the high hurdles and long relay. Dave Nolan eased round in the single lap doing just enough to win the 400, nursing a split ankle bone which was to require surgery a fortnight later. Junior Michael Parper excelled in his first league appearance and should have finished second in the 'B' 400m but was impeded by the starter standing in his lane in the finishing straight.

Matt Yates had two fine wins in the 'A' 800m and 1500m, while Paul Evans stamped his mark on the 1500m and 5000m, running in with Gary Staines in the latter event. Add to this the dream team of Justin Chaston and Darren Mead in the 3000m steeplechase and you can see that a middle distance squad doesn't come much better than this.

Added to our injury woes was Kevin Brown. Kevin who has been such a loyal and enthusiastic addition to our throws squad insisted on throwing despite injury. Leith Marar took advantage to throw a season's best (until the Cup Final), and Kevin who also competed in the shot was destined for major surgery a week later. Ian Tullett was sidelined from the pole vault, yet another key man awaiting surgery. With so many of the team giving and willing to give 110%, the few non-appearances throughout the season were thrown into sharp perspective as the rest of the team roared on every effort.

By the last event, the announcer had declared Belgrave clear victors. Dave Nolan was dropped from the long relay to prevent further damage to his leg and we gracefully received the trophy, the accolades and commiserated with Sale.

The Sale team had just boarded their coach when an embarrassed League Secretary dropped the bombshell. The pole vault had been misscored, the Sale 'B' competitor's score had been omitted. Yes, Belgrave were still match winners but only by 8 points. We had failed to overhaul Sale's accumulative score by a mere 3 points. President Snowy Brooks was gracious as he presented the now jubilant Sale squad with that League Trophy. For them a trip in '97 to Moscow, for us the chance of revenge in the Cup Final.

Track chat: When Ian Wells insisted on running the 3000m steeplechase in Match 1, his reasoning was that the league title would be settled eventually by the odd point or two. He was right and although he and many other team members did everything possible to amass points through the season, an unusual series of disasters plus conflicting competitions meant that once again we had just failed to gain the ultimate T&F team prize - Champions of the British Athletic League.

Report · page 19

Semi-Final, Wavertree, Liverpool, 28 Jul 1996: Demented "Wellsey" saves the day!

Guardian Insurance Gold Cup

Demented "Wellsey" saves the day!

Our second visit within 2 months to Wavertree Athletics Centre, Liverpool, nearly proved a disaster. But as usual Ian Wells helped to save the day. This time as self appointed team manager. (Another event to add to his ever growing list. )

So what happened? Well ... the team travelled up the day before and enjoyed a leisurely breakfast before the advance squad set off for the track. As Bill Laws' car entered the car park a demented figure in claret and gold flung himself on the bonnet. "I've entered Phil Spivey - they're in to the second round of the hammer. Better move quick." As the timetable which Belgrave had received indicated the hammer was not due to start for 1½ hours, this was greeted with some derision. But Ian's demeanour bordering on the manic, put a quick end to the frivolity.

Phil Spivey, Throws Captain and Mister Unflappable, moved purposely to the circle. He wasn't there to mess about and two rounds later he took the lead for maximum points. So what had gone wrong? After much scratching around it appeared that we had been sent the timetable for the second round fixture, which was considerably different from the one for the semi. A quick scurry back to the hotel to mobilise the rest of the team had some effect although a few were still sightseeing. Back to the track and disaster was averted - well nearly. Unfortunately, we hadn't got the message to Jim Estall who arrived for the 10,000m as the leaders entered their 3rd lap. After agitated protests Jim was allowed to run, but was not allowed to score. Our protest was not upheld. Soon after, Mike Edwards no heighted in the Pole Vault - thankfully a rare happening - but the tension grew. Neil Owen and Chris Rawlinson swapped the long and sprint hurdles due to Chris' continuing injury which meant he only had one good lead leg. Both scored good points with Chris' win being a bonus. Solid performances from the rest of our regulars helped us move into second place and a chance to put matters right in the Cup Final itself. Wins from Kevin Brown, Dave Nolan, Nigel Bevan, Kassa Tadesse, made the difference.

Report · page 19

Final, Copthall, 31 Aug 1996: A golden finish to the season

A golden finish to the season

After 20 years sponsoring the British Athletics Leagues and Cup Competitions, this was to be Guardian Assurance's last match. The 1996 Gold Cup Final was to be a special event and with little sign of further financial support, we wondered what the future format would be.

So everyone was determined to make it a big occasion and, once again, thanks to the wholehearted support of our middle distance squad, we triumphed for our third Cup win.

Pride of place went to our distance men with wins by Matt Yates, Justin Chaston, Paul Evans and Gary Staines. Neil Owen blew the opposition away for another 110mH victory. Junior Geoffrey Ojok had a remarkable victory over Linford Christie, not unfortunately in the 100m, but in Linford's new event - the long jump. We're not quite certain how TVH's specialist jumper felt as he watched Linford struggle to 6.67m, but we do know that Geoffrey was determined to gain his scalp even if he himself was finding it difficult to hit his correct stride patten Linford made amends in the 100m with a new Cup record of 10.28 secs, but the Man of the Match Award went to Bob Weir of Birchfield who broke the Cup discus record with an impressive 62.40. Leith Marar threw a season's best to gain an impressive second place.

Once again, our Young Athletes made an impressive contribution with Richard David easing his way to 48.85 in the flat 400m. Philip Idowu looked tired at the end of a tong season but Marlon Dickson looked better than ever, running both relays. Even he was surprised how easy a 49.1 leg felt.

So, victory to the Bels with a comfortable 9 points margin over Woodford Green. We had expected Newham & Essex to be the main threat but Woodford had been recruiting strongly. After several years where they have floated between Divisions 1 and 2, they now look like a force to be reckoned with. Sale slumped to 5th, clearly unable to raise their game after their league victory, and Haringey, who had been relegated to Division 2 for 1997 suffered the ignominy of finishing last.

Records · page 20

Belgrave's 1996 British Athletics League Rankings by Event

Bill Laws

Over the last few years our Heavy Squad has provided the muscle from which a series of League and Cup title wins have developed. Last year Dave Smith and Lee Newman were welcome additions, while at the tail end of the 1996 season Matt Simson joined Belgrave to further strengthen an already impressive throws squad. Their contribution to our League and Cup successes may be judged from the following table which aggregates League Div. 1 points for A and B strings and then divides the totals by the maximum possible points to give the % performance and hence Ranking positions.

The sprint and long relays are limited to 1 event each per Match, so that the maximum points possible for these events in 1 season are 64, while all other events could score 112 maximum. This is worked out on the basis that an A String win is worth 16 points and a B String win 12 points.

For budding statisticians the complete scoring for a British League fixture is as follows :-

A String: 16,14,12,10,8,6,5,4. B String: 12,10, 8, 6,4,3,2,1.

Photograph · page 20

It's great to have such people as Gill Fairclough to rely upon. Gill has been associated with Belgrave for many years, husband Tony being one of our star cross country runners from the '60s. Both Gill and Tony have gone on to put something back into the sport and do a great deal of officiating, particularly in Kent. At our home McDonald's YA Meeting at Crystal Palace Gill took on the arduous role of Field Events Referee while Tony was Track Ref. What would we have done without them?
